# Tactic and simplifier proof-support review

**Review revision:** `4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf`
(`dt2bv-certified-proof-converter`, PR #11513).
This reviews the checkout containing the audited `dt2bv` converter, not subsequent
changes on `origin/master`. No tactic or simplifier implementation is changed by
this document.

## Classification and scope

| Class | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **1** | No proof-production change identified for the reviewed, admitted path. Existing assertion proofs, valid primitive equalities, definitional extensions or converters suffice. A deliberately read-only/non-refuting tactic also belongs here. |
| **2a** | A proof-producing path logs an invalid equality, mismatched inference, missing premise or incorrect certificate. |
| **2b** | A proof-producing/accepting path lacks original-premise/refutation transport, leaves new asserted premises, or accepts a proof request without producing its required refutation. |
| **3** | The transformation does not currently support proofs, but local certificates, existing rewrite/definition rules, or a straightforward transport/fallback can add support. |
| **4** | The transformation does not support proofs and needs substantial new evidence, projection/elimination/encoding calculus, contextual provenance or backend reconstruction. |

Classes 2a and 2b can coexist. Confirmed defective parameter variants take
precedence over a safe default in the catalogue; the default and relevant
restrictions are stated explicitly. Classes 3 and 4 describe the actual
transformation, **not** success of an identity operation in proof mode.
Difficulty is an engineering judgment, with the missing work recorded for each
entry. A proof-aware fallback can be easy even when certifying the original
backend remains difficult.

Factory class 1 is conditional on the stated backend/primitive contract.
Untriggered defensive-branch concerns and source-only parameter reachability are
identified separately rather than treated as demonstrated failures of every
factory using SMT.

The catalogue covers **every registered public name: 125 tactics and 45
simplifiers**, including aliases and old/new implementations sharing similar
names. Registration extraction was compared with the local runtime catalogue.
The additional helper catalogue covers directly associated unregistered
simplifiers, rewriters, encoders and driver boundaries. It is not an independent
verification of every mathematical identity or every solver/theory implementation
transitively called by these frontends.

This is a source-level correctness review, **not a mechanized proof of all C++**.
Class 1 means that no necessary producer change was identified under its stated
contract; it does not assert that Pulse already checks every emitted rule.
The `dt2bv` audit has its own explicitly conditional semantic guarantees in
`audits\dt2bv-proof-converter\REPORT.md`.

### Proof contract used in the review

An assertion entry `(f, p)` must satisfy `fact(p) = f`, with `p` depending only on
original assumptions and explicitly justified extensions. A same-signature
rewrite requires an equality from the old expression to the new expression,
composed with the original assertion by modus ponens. Null must not mean both
"identity" and "a changed expression whose proof is missing".

`mk_rewrite(old, new)` is not inherently defective: a universally valid equality
is a legitimate primitive certificate. It is defective when the equality needs
an unrecorded premise, a fresh-symbol correspondence or a missing side condition.
Likewise a closed, correct quantified-elimination equivalence can be a valid
primitive; replacing a quantified formula by a mathematically wrong result cannot.

**Observational equivalence is different:** `OP_OEQ` (`~`) denotes
equisatisfiability modulo namings, and `PR_REWRITE` and
`PR_MODUS_PONENS_OEQ` explicitly admit it
(`src\api\z3_api.h:208-209,511-516,727-738`). A valid fresh-witness extension can
use that calculus without an additional converter. A countervaluation to
pointwise `F = G` does not disprove `F ~ G`. The checker must validate the actual
extension/witness and scope contract; it must not silently interpret `~` as `=`.

Fresh encodings must use checked definition/extension rules or a converter that
closes the encoding over original expressions and restores original-premise
ownership. A **model converter is not a proof converter**. Generated range or
congruence constraints cannot silently become new original asserted premises.
Whole-proof transport must also visit expression-valued declaration parameters,
such as quantifier-instantiation bindings, and preserve binder sorts and scope.

Approximation precision is part of the contract. `UNDER` goals cannot certify
original UNSAT; `OVER` goals cannot certify original SAT. The current decision
predicates enforce this in `src\tactic\goal.cpp:634-647`. Arbitrary `add-bounds`
constraints therefore describe bounded search, not original theorems.

## Cross-cutting findings

### Unsupported tactic simplifiers and custom solver pipelines differ

`dependent_expr_state_tactic` checks `supports_proofs()` before reducing a
proof-enabled goal (`src\tactic\dependent_expr_state_tactic.h:124-146`).
Unsupported reductions are silently skipped. This protects those tactic
adapters, but it does not make the underlying simplifier proof-capable.

The custom solver API has no equivalent gate:
`src\api\api_solver.cpp:287-312` installs a factory;
`src\solver\simplifier_solver.cpp:230-235` accepts it;
the pipeline executes reductions directly. Reconstruction
(`src\solver\simplifier_solver.cpp:305-336`) skips missing proofs and substitutes
supplied proofs for asserted leaves without checking that their facts match.

**Confirmed 2a/2b:** `randomizer` in this unchecked path retains original proofs
while renaming the formulas. The returned proof combines `asserted(x > 0)` with
a rewrite whose left side is `x_rand > 0`. Merely replacing the asserted leaf is
not inverse-renaming the whole proof. Other proofless passes can instead leave
transformed/new asserted leaves. Enforce the capability contract at construction
and composition, reject mismatched proof facts, and provide actual converters
before allowing signature-changing reductions.

### Proof availability must be enforced at solver-to-tactic boundaries

The registered sequential `qffd` and `smtfd` paths can accept proof-enabled CNF
goals and return UNSAT without a refutation. Their backends return null proofs,
and `src\solver\solver2tactic.cpp:119-148` still publishes the false goal.
This is a confirmed **2b availability/transport boundary defect**, not a claim
that those backends already have proofs and merely forgot one converter.
Gate these paths or select a proof-capable backend; full backend support remains
category 4 work.

There is a further **source-reviewed generic transport concern**:
`solver2tactic` asserts extracted formulas without their goal proofs and
replaces an existing converter with a constant backend proof converter
(`src\solver\solver2tactic.cpp:30-78,104-122`). A proof-capable use of this wrapper
on a previously transformed goal needs original-premise replacement and
composition with the incoming converter. No additional public proof-capable
backend reproduction is claimed here.

## Producer defects and conditional source findings

The records below distinguish malformed inferences, false rewrite identities and
missing conversion. Reproductions inspect returned proof objects, not merely
satisfiability of a reasserted residual goal. The SMT certificate bailout is a
conditional source finding, explicitly not a triggered runtime reproduction.

<!-- BEGIN FINDINGS -->
### CORE-BB-FRESH: bit-blast logs original BV constant equal to unrelated fresh bits

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

mk_const creates fresh Boolean constants and returns mkbv(tuple) without a definition proof. process_const synthesizes rewrite(= x (mkbv tuple)). This equality is not universally valid: x=#b00,k0=true,k1=false is a countervaluation. A fresh-symbol encoding is equisatisfiable but needs definitions or inverse theorem transport. The later MP correctly links endpoints only relative to this invalid certificate; the model converter does not repair it.

**Witness / observation:** Existing-build Python: import z3; z3.set_param(proof=True); x=z3.BitVec('x',2); s=z3.Then('bit-blast','smt').solver(); s.add(x==0,x==1); print(s.check()); print(s.proof().sexpr()). Actual: unsat; proof contains (rewrite (= x (mkbv k!0 k!1))) under monotonicity, then resolves not k!0 and k!0. No intro-def/apply-def or proof converter closes fresh bits. Source HEAD independently contains this same mechanism; binary build hash is different (see validation).

**Source:** [`src\ast\rewriter\bit_blaster\bit_blaster_rewriter.cpp:229-254`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/rewriter/bit_blaster/bit_blaster_rewriter.cpp#L229-L254); [`src\ast\rewriter\bit_blaster\bit_blaster_rewriter.cpp:403-416`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/rewriter/bit_blaster/bit_blaster_rewriter.cpp#L403-L416); [`src\ast\rewriter\rewriter_def.h:79-120`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/rewriter/rewriter_def.h#L79-L120); [`src\tactic\bv\bit_blaster_tactic.cpp:72-101`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/bv/bit_blaster_tactic.cpp#L72-L101)

### CORE-FPA-FRESH: fpa2bv treats fresh FP/RM encodings as unconditional equalities

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

FP/RM constants are replaced by fp(extract freshBV) or rm(freshBV), while no definition proof or inverse PC is supplied. The framework emits rewrite equating original and independent fresh decoding. Choose original f as NaN and fresh bits as +0: the equality is false. Original rounding mode RNE and fresh bits #b001 similarly disagree. Numeric operation rewrites themselves are not implicated merely by their use of rewrite.

**Witness / observation:** Existing-build Python: z3.set_param(proof=True); f=z3.FP('f',z3.Float32()); s=z3.Then('fpa2bv','smt').solver(); s.add(z3.fpIsNaN(f),z3.Not(z3.fpIsNaN(f))); s.check() returned unsat. s.proof().sexpr() contained (rewrite (= f (fp ((_ extract 31 31) k!1) ((_ extract 30 23) k!1) ((_ extract 22 0) k!1)))) and no defining certificate. Rounding-mode example in CORE-FPA-EXTRAS also contains rewrite(= r (rm k!3)).

**Source:** [`src\ast\fpa\fpa2bv_converter.cpp:193-235`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/fpa/fpa2bv_converter.cpp#L193-L235); [`src\ast\fpa\fpa2bv_converter.cpp:337-361`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/fpa/fpa2bv_converter.cpp#L337-L361); [`src\ast\fpa\fpa2bv_rewriter.cpp:61-70`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/fpa/fpa2bv_rewriter.cpp#L61-L70); [`src\ast\rewriter\rewriter_def.h:79-120`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/rewriter/rewriter_def.h#L79-L120); [`src\tactic\fpa\fpa2bv_tactic.cpp:63-72`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/fpa/fpa2bv_tactic.cpp#L63-L72); [`src\tactic\fpa\fpa2bv_tactic.cpp:90-105`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/fpa/fpa2bv_tactic.cpp#L90-L105)

### CORE-FPA-EXTRAS: fpa2bv extra encoding constraints survive as unoriginal asserted premises

**Class:** 2b. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

The tactic attaches only mk_fpa2bv_model_converter, then inserts each converter extra expression using mk_asserted. For a rounding-mode constant this includes freshBV<=4. No original-premise proof, defining extension certificate, or PC replaces that asserted leaf. A refutation can depend on it, proving the augmented encoding instead of returning a theorem whose asserted leaves are the original input formulas. This gap is independent of the invalid fresh equality logger.

**Witness / observation:** Existing-build Python: z3.set_param(proof=True); r=z3.Const('r',z3.RNE().sort()); modes=[z3.RNE(),z3.RNA(),z3.RTP(),z3.RTN(),z3.RTZ()]; s=z3.Then('fpa2bv','smt').solver(); s.add(*[r!=v for v in modes]); s.check() returned unsat. s.proof().sexpr() contained (asserted (bvule k!3 #b100)) as a needed unit-resolution premise, although none of the five original assertions mentions k!3 or any BV bound.

**Source:** [`src\tactic\fpa\fpa2bv_tactic.cpp:90-105`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/fpa/fpa2bv_tactic.cpp#L90-L105); [`src\ast\fpa\fpa2bv_converter.cpp:337-361`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/fpa/fpa2bv_converter.cpp#L337-L361); [`src\ast\fpa\fpa2bv_converter.cpp:269-325`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/fpa/fpa2bv_converter.cpp#L269-L325)

### CORE-NNF-SK-HACK: sk_hack parameter strengthens an existential without its predicate premise

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

When sk_hack=true and an existential carries a one-argument Boolean pattern whose declaration is named sk_hack, skolemizer changes body P(x) to P(x) and sk_hack(x) before substituting the witness. It still emits mk_skolemization(original_exists,strengthened_body), although SMT quantifier patterns impose no logical predicate assumption. Thus even a complete refutation can be of a satisfiable original formula.

**Witness / observation:** Existing-build Python: z3.set_param(proof=True); x=z3.Int('x'); h=z3.Function('sk_hack',z3.IntSort(),z3.BoolSort()); fs=[z3.Exists(x,x==0,patterns=[h(x)]),z3.ForAll(x,z3.Not(h(x)))]. Ordinary Solver(fs) returned sat. Both Then(With(Tactic('snf'),sk_hack=True),'smt') and corresponding nnf solver returned unsat. Translated goal includes x!n=0 and sk_hack(x!n); proof contains sk(~ (exists ((x Int)) (! (= x 0) :pattern ((sk_hack x)))) (and (= x!n 0) (sk_hack x!n))). Default sk_hack=false is unaffected.

**Source:** [`src\ast\normal_forms\nnf.cpp:126-158`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/normal_forms/nnf.cpp#L126-L158); [`src\ast\normal_forms\nnf.cpp:191-208`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/normal_forms/nnf.cpp#L191-L208); [`src\ast\normal_forms\nnf.cpp:302-324`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/normal_forms/nnf.cpp#L302-L324); [`src\tactic\core\nnf_tactic.cpp:54-83`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/core/nnf_tactic.cpp#L54-L83); [`src\tactic\core\nnf_tactic.cpp:104-113`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/core/nnf_tactic.cpp#L104-L113)

### CORE-FD-NO-PROOF: Sequential FD tactic factories accept proof goals but return false with no refutation

**Class:** 2b. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

qffd/smtfd factories expose solver2tactic without a proof gate or supported fallback. The underlying backends return nullptr from get_proof_core. Direct CNF incremental-SAT internalization bypasses the non-CNF proof refusal. Consequently a proof-enabled goal can be returned as false without a refutation of its original assertions. The backend is genuinely unsupported (category4 engineering effort), but this reachable public acceptance is a missing-proof boundary defect, not a defect inferred from a simplifier supports_proofs flag. Parent owns generic solver2tactic and custom-simplifier-driver analysis.

**Witness / observation:** Existing-build Python: z3.set_param(proof=True); p=z3.Bool('p'); for name in ['qffd','smtfd']: g=z3.Goal(proofs=True); g.add(p,z3.Not(p)); print(z3.Tactic(name)(g).sexpr()) returned (goals (goal false)); s=z3.Tactic(name).solver(); s.add(p,z3.Not(p)); s.check() returned unsat; s.proof() raised 'there is no current proof'. Non-CNF BV examples correctly raised 'generation of proof objects is not supported in this mode'. pqffd on an unresolved goal correctly raised 'parallel_tactic does not support proof production'.

**Source:** [`src\tactic\fd_solver\fd_solver.cpp:29-48`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/fd_solver/fd_solver.cpp#L29-L48); [`src\sat\sat_solver\inc_sat_solver.cpp:483-485`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/sat/sat_solver/inc_sat_solver.cpp#L483-L485); [`src\sat\sat_solver\inc_sat_solver.cpp:875-897`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/sat/sat_solver/inc_sat_solver.cpp#L875-L897); [`src\sat\sat_solver\inc_sat_solver.cpp:1098-1124`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/sat/sat_solver/inc_sat_solver.cpp#L1098-L1124); [`src\tactic\fd_solver\smtfd_solver.cpp:2095-2096`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/fd_solver/smtfd_solver.cpp#L2095-L2096); [`src\tactic\fd_solver\smtfd_solver.cpp:2139-2145`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/fd_solver/smtfd_solver.cpp#L2139-L2145); [`src\solver\solver2tactic.cpp:119-166`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/solver/solver2tactic.cpp#L119-L166)

### ARITH-QE-BIT2INT-ZERO: QE internal bit2int preprocessing turns a SAT zero-divisor formula into false

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

Parent proved that bit2int reduces Int mod(BV2Int(u),BV2Int(v)) to BV unsigned remainder without v!=0. QE explicitly forces m_simplify_bit2int=true in eliminate_block, so this is reachable through the registered qe tactic even without an unchecked custom user simplifier. Int mod0 is underspecified and may return 0 for dividend 1; BV UREM(1,0) returns 1. Therefore Exists(x, u=1 and v=0 and x=mod(BV2Int(u),BV2Int(v)) and x!=BV2Int(u)) is SAT with x=0, but internal preprocessing refutes it and qe emits a false whole-quantifier equivalence. This is a concrete semantic error, not objection to using PR_REWRITE for correct QE equivalence.

**Witness / observation:** Python proof mode: u,v=BitVecs('u v',4); x=Int('x'); f=Exists(x,And(u==1,v==0,x==BV2Int(u)%BV2Int(v),x!=BV2Int(u))). Ordinary Solver().add(f); check() -> sat, model [v=0,u=1,mod0=[else->0]]. Goal(proofs=True), Tactic('qe')(goal) -> [[False]] in the first run. Then('qe','smt').solver() -> unsat; actual proof and-elim(mp(asserted(f),rewrite(f == And(u==1,v==0,False)),And(u==1,v==0,False)),False). qe-light(proofs=True) instead leaves f unchanged and its SMT pipeline returns SAT. Existing-build hash differs from HEAD as documented.

**Source:** [`src\qe\qe.cpp:2120-2148`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/qe/qe.cpp#L2120-L2148); [`src\qe\qe.cpp:1432-1463`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/qe/qe.cpp#L1432-L1463); [`src\qe\qe_tactic.cpp:63-71`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/qe/qe_tactic.cpp#L63-L71); [`src\ast\rewriter\bit2int.cpp:347-372`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/rewriter/bit2int.cpp#L347-L372); [`src\solver\solver_preprocess.cpp:78`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/solver/solver_preprocess.cpp#L78); [`src\solver\assertions\asserted_formulas.h:209`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/solver/assertions/asserted_formulas.h#L209)

### ARITH-FIX-DL-ZERO: Gauge fixing emits invalid unconditional zero-substitution rewrites

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

Difference-logic formulas can be satisfiability-equivalent under a simultaneous translation/gauge choice, but x+(-1)*y <= 1 is not pointwise equivalent to x <= 1. subst.insert(y,0) supplies no proof of y=0, and the resulting certificate fills surrounding reductions with ordinary rewrites such as (-1*y)=0 and (y+(-1)*x)=(-1*x). Those equalities are false for y=1. No gauge hypothesis or converter discharges the choice; model converter alone cannot justify the proof.

**Witness / observation:** Python: z3.set_param(proof=True); x,y=Ints('x y'); s=Then('fix-dl-var','smt').solver(); s.add(x+(-1)*y<=1,y+(-1)*x<=-2); s.check() -> unsat. Actual proof contains mp(asserted(x+-1*y<=1),monotonicity(...rewrite(-1*y==0)...),x<=1) and rewrite(y+-1*x==-1*x). Goal(proofs=True) residual is x<=1,x>=2. The original contradiction is real, but the returned derivation contains invalid rewrite facts.

**Source:** [`src\tactic\arith\fix_dl_var_tactic.cpp:247-275`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/arith/fix_dl_var_tactic.cpp#L247-L275); [`src\ast\rewriter\th_rewriter.cpp:1027-1034`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/rewriter/th_rewriter.cpp#L1027-L1034); [`src\ast\rewriter\rewriter_def.h:147-156`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/rewriter/rewriter_def.h#L147-L156); [`src\ast\rewriter\rewriter_def.h:360-373`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/rewriter/rewriter_def.h#L360-L373)

### ARITH-PURIFY-MOD-QUOTIENT: Modulo purification defines the quotient as another remainder

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

process_mod calls process_idiv with the original OP_MOD declaration. process_idiv constructs div_app with that declaration and therefore proves k1=mod(x,y), while mod_pr proves k2=mod(x,y). It then emits the Euclidean decomposition y=0 or x=k1*y+k2 from both proofs. At x=5,y=2 the premises force k1=k2=1 but the conclusion is 5=3, so this is a concrete invalid arithmetic lemma. The residual SAT encoding can still use k1 as an unconstrained quotient because definitions exist only in proof evidence; this finding concerns proof correctness rather than an observed incorrect SAT result.

**Witness / observation:** Python proof mode: x=Int('x'); s=Then('purify-arith','smt').solver(); s.add(x==5,x%2==0); s.check() -> unsat. Traversing actual proof yielded th-lemma(apply-def(intro-def(x%2==k!2),~(x%2,k!2)),apply-def(intro-def(x%2==k!3),~(x%2,k!3)),Or(2==0,x==k!2*2+k!3)). Direct residual: x==5,k!1==0,x==2*k!0+k!1,0<=k!1,Not(2<=k!1).

**Source:** [`src\tactic\arith\purify_arith_tactic.cpp:349-377`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/arith/purify_arith_tactic.cpp#L349-L377); [`src\tactic\arith\purify_arith_tactic.cpp:407-414`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/arith/purify_arith_tactic.cpp#L407-L414); [`src\tactic\arith\purify_arith_tactic.cpp:728-730`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/arith/purify_arith_tactic.cpp#L728-L730)

### ARITH-PURIFY-CONGRUENCE-LEAF: Purified operator congruence becomes a new asserted premise

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

Pairwise div/mod/idiv congruence constraints mention fresh purified outputs. They follow from equality of original operator applications plus their fresh definitions, but are not tautologies in the fresh outputs. All three loops use assert_expr without a proof. Returned refutations can therefore retain an asserted leaf that is neither an original input nor a discharged extension definition. Need congruence/transitivity with both apply-def proofs, not an asserted premise.

**Witness / observation:** Python: a,b,u,v=Reals('a b u v'); s=Then('purify-arith','smt').solver(); s.add(a==b,u==v,u==0,a/u!=b/v); s.check() -> unsat. Traversal of asserted proof leaves returned ['a/u != b/v','u == v','a == b','Implies(And(a == b,u == v),k!2 == k!3)']. The last leaf is newly generated and lacks derivation; u==0 was not needed by this particular final proof.

**Source:** [`src\tactic\arith\purify_arith_tactic.cpp:279-285`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/arith/purify_arith_tactic.cpp#L279-L285); [`src\tactic\arith\purify_arith_tactic.cpp:825-852`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/arith/purify_arith_tactic.cpp#L825-L852)

### ARITH-PURIFY-FRESH-THEORY-LEMMA: Trigonometric and zero-power lemmas omit required fresh definitions

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

The basis conversion makes x=sin(theta), y=cos(theta), but the unit-circle lemma is emitted from only the current operator's definition. Given just x=sin(theta), the fact 1=x*x+y*y is false at theta=0,x=0,y=0; the missing cosine definition is essential. Separately, the x^0 branch creates an entirely fresh shared p0 and emits x!=0 or k=p0 from only k=x^0, without defining p0=0^0. Its conclusion does not follow from its recorded premise. Non-basis sin/cos inserts an asserted unit-circle axiom as a lemma premise instead of a zero-premise arithmetic/theory axiom.

**Witness / observation:** Proof mode SMT-LIB input: (declare-const theta Real)(assert (= (+ (* (sin theta) (sin theta)) (* (cos theta) (cos theta))) 0)). Then(purify-arith,smt) -> unsat. Actual proof contains th-lemma(apply-def(intro-def(sin(theta)==k!8),~(sin(theta),k!8)),1==k!8*k!8+k!9*k!9), with no cosine definition among this lemma's parents. Second probe x=0,y=0,distinct(^x 0,^y 0) -> unsat, proof contains th-lemma(apply-def(intro-def(x**0==k!6),~(x**0,k!6)),Or(Not(x==0),k!6==k!7)); k!7 is the undefined shared fresh zero-power constant.

**Source:** [`src\tactic\arith\purify_arith_tactic.cpp:461-479`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/arith/purify_arith_tactic.cpp#L461-L479); [`src\tactic\arith\purify_arith_tactic.cpp:564-588`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/arith/purify_arith_tactic.cpp#L564-L588); [`src\tactic\arith\purify_arith_tactic.cpp:288-296`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/arith/purify_arith_tactic.cpp#L288-L296)

### LOGICS-HORN-TRANSPORT: Horn engine proof is not transported to the original goal

**Class:** 2b. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

Original per-formula proofs never enter fixedpoint add_rule. Normalization and fresh-query rules become independent asserted leaves. Constant proof2proof_converter captures the engine proof, but neither maps these leaves to original assumptions nor discharges the synthesized query. A proof of a fresh query is not a refutation; an asserted false has introduced false as an assumption.

**Witness / observation:** Python, local build 5.3.4, set_param(proof=True): Then('simplify','horn','smt').solver() on a, Implies(a,b), Not(b) returns unsat, proof sexpr '(asserted false)'. With(Tactic('horn'),engine='bmc') on a,Not(a), followed by smt returns unsat with proof ending query!6, not false; fresh query implication is an asserted leaf. No artifact changes/builds.

**Source:** [`src\muz\fp\horn_tactic.cpp:177-246`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/muz/fp/horn_tactic.cpp#L177-L246); [`src\muz\fp\horn_tactic.cpp:268-275`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/muz/fp/horn_tactic.cpp#L268-L275); [`src\muz\base\dl_context.cpp:460-475`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/muz/base/dl_context.cpp#L460-L475); [`src\muz\spacer\spacer_sat_answer.cpp:109-113`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/muz/spacer/spacer_sat_answer.cpp#L109-L113)

### LOGICS-QUASI-MACRO-DEF-AXIOM: Quasi-macro proof redefines an original function as an unconditional definition axiom

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

Only the else declaration is fresh. The axiom defines ORIGINAL h(x,X0)=ite(X0=0,x,h!else(x,X0)); at X0=0 it asserts h(x,0)=x, which is not a tautology or a definition of a fresh symbol. The code never uses the proof of the original quantified premise to justify this restriction. For x=0 it alone contradicts h(0,0)!=0. The reproduced proof's asserted input leaves omit ForAll(x,h(x,0)=x), demonstrating incorrect assumption provenance independently of checker support.

**Witness / observation:** set_param(proof=True); x=Int('x'); h=Function('h',IntSort(),IntSort(),IntSort()); s=Then('quasi-macros','smt').solver(); s.add(ForAll([x],h(x,0)==x),h(0,0)!=0); check() is unsat. proof contains '(def-axiom (forall ((X0 Int) (x Int)) (= (h x X0) (ite (and (= X0 0)) x (h!else!5 x X0)))))' and only asserted '(distinct (h 0 0) 0)', no asserted original forall.

**Source:** [`src\ast\macros\quasi_macros.cpp:254-271`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/macros/quasi_macros.cpp#L254-L271); [`src\ast\macros\quasi_macros.cpp:297-305`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/macros/quasi_macros.cpp#L297-L305); [`src\ast\macros\quasi_macros.cpp:349-360`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/macros/quasi_macros.cpp#L349-L360); [`src\tactic\ufbv\quasi_macros_tactic.cpp:70-88`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/tactic/ufbv/quasi_macros_tactic.cpp#L70-L88)

### LOGICS-SMT-FABRICATED-FALSE-BAILOUT: Sequential SMT invents asserted false when the solver certificate is absent or has the wrong fact

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** source-reviewed.

On l_false, lines 270-271 explicitly replace a null certificate or a certificate whose fact is not false with mk_asserted(false). This introduces false as a new premise instead of deriving it from input proofs, allowing tactic consumers to receive a purported refutation without any proof of the original goal. Correct behavior is certificate materialization/transport or refusal. This is a conditional source defect, not evidence that ordinary SMT always takes the bailout.

**Witness / observation:** No triggered bailout reproduction. Ordinary proof-enabled Boolean and LIA contradictions, including clause_proof=true, produced valid-looking proof-trail conclusions false and did NOT trigger the branch. Classification is source-reviewed and conditional on missing/wrong-conclusion backend certificate.

**Source:** [`src\smt\tactic\smt_tactic_core.cpp:244-275`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/smt/tactic/smt_tactic_core.cpp#L244-L275)

### P1: Unchecked custom simplifier pipelines can return malformed proofs

**Class:** 2a/2b. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

A proof-enabled wrapper runs an unsupported randomizer. Reconstruction replaces asserted renamed facts with stale original proofs while leaving the remaining proof over renamed symbols. Modus-ponens antecedents then disagree. For entries with missing proofs, reconstruction may instead retain unoriginal asserted premises. The adapter's support gate is not present in this API path.

**Witness / observation:** Context(proof=True); Simplifier('randomizer').add(Solver()); assert x>0,x<0; UNSAT proof contains asserted(> x 0) combined with rewrite((> x_rand 0)=(not (<= x_rand 0))).

**Source:** [`src\api\api_solver.cpp:287-312`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/api/api_solver.cpp#L287-L312); [`src\solver\simplifier_solver.cpp:230-235`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/solver/simplifier_solver.cpp#L230-L235); [`src\solver\simplifier_solver.cpp:305-336`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/solver/simplifier_solver.cpp#L305-L336)

### P2: Demodulation changes non-ground terms without its defining proof

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

Non-ground instance construction returns null but the match is still applied. Missing old-to-new body evidence is composed with elim-unused as if it were identity. Returned proof proves false from the second quantified premise alone; that premise is satisfiable with f(x)=x+1.

**Witness / observation:** Both a demodulator simplifier solver and Then('demodulator','smt').solver(), in a proof context, on forall x:Int f(x)=x and forall x:Int f(x)>x produce `(mp (asserted (forall x (> (f x) x))) (elim-unused (= (forall x false) false)) false)`.

**Source:** [`src\ast\simplifiers\demodulator_simplifier.cpp:143-159`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/simplifiers/demodulator_simplifier.cpp#L143-L159); [`src\ast\simplifiers\demodulator_simplifier.cpp:205-213`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/simplifiers/demodulator_simplifier.cpp#L205-L213); [`src\ast\substitution\demodulator_rewriter.cpp:278-337`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/substitution/demodulator_rewriter.cpp#L278-L337)

### P3: Existential quantifier pulling drops its rewrite certificate

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

mk_rewrite is called but its result is not assigned to result_pr. The surrounding substitution receives no old-to-new equality, and the final MP's proof antecedent is the new negated-forall rather than the old exists.

**Witness / observation:** Proof-enabled pull-nested-quantifiers simplifier solver on Exists(x:Bool,f(x)) and ForAll(x:Bool,Not(f(x))) produces an MP of asserted Exists(x,f(x)) with rewrite((not forall x not f(x))=(not forall x not f(x))).

**Source:** [`src\ast\normal_forms\pull_quant.cpp:281-286`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/normal_forms/pull_quant.cpp#L281-L286); [`src\ast\simplifiers\pull_nested_quantifiers.h:32-47`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/simplifiers/pull_nested_quantifiers.h#L32-L47)

### P4: bit2int leaks a previous formula's proof into unchanged inputs

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

The output proof reference is not reset on unchanged input, while the simplifier reuses it across formulas and calls MP even for unchanged formulas. Consequently an arithmetic assertion is paired with an unrelated BV-to-Int equality.

**Witness / observation:** Proof-enabled bit2int simplifier solver on BV2Int(u:BV4)==1, a:Int>0, a<0 returns a proof with four MP endpoint mismatches.

**Source:** [`src\ast\simplifiers\bit2int.h:32-38`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/simplifiers/bit2int.h#L32-L38); [`src\ast\rewriter\bit2int.cpp:32-40`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/rewriter/bit2int.cpp#L32-L40)

### P5: bit2int treats integer mod-by-zero as BV remainder-by-zero

**Class:** 2a. **Evidence status:** confirmed.

Integer mod by zero is underspecified and may return an arbitrary completion value. BV unsigned remainder by zero returns the dividend. No divisor-nonzero premise or completion-preserving branch is recorded, so the generated unconditional rewrite equality is false.

**Witness / observation:** u:BV4=1, v:BV4=0, BV2Int(u)%BV2Int(v)!=BV2Int(u): ordinary proof-enabled Solver SAT with mod0 else=0; bit2int simplifier solver UNSAT. The rewrite itself changes satisfiability, not merely checker coverage.

**Source:** [`src\ast\rewriter\bit2int.cpp:347-372`](https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3/blob/4f38d9c769688e49cf45f49d0b98ebfc063f87cf/src/ast/rewriter/bit2int.cpp#L347-L372)

## Public tactic catalogue

An adapter row inherits the reviewed target simplifier's classification, not the
adapter's successful proof-mode no-op. Portfolio rows include reachable defective
preprocessors; a correct final SMT backend cannot repair an invalid upstream
certificate.

| `blast-term-ite` | **3** | Adapter to simplifier `blast-term-ite`. Distributes an application over a non-Boolean ite argument, f(...,ite(c,t,e),...) to ite(c,f(...,t,...),f(...,e,...)), or removes the argument ite when both branches are equal. No fresh symbols: m_num_fresh is an inflation counter, not declaration creation. Its rewriter already enables proof generation and the simplifier composes returned proofs with the input proof. | `elim-uncnstr2` | **4** | Adapter to simplifier `elim-unconstrained`. Reference-count/root-DAG elimination identifies singly used unfrozen ground constants, replaces invertible parent terms by fresh terms, reconstructs ancestors and installs inverse model definitions. The inverter handles arithmetic, BV, arrays, datatypes, sequences and finite sets, including restricted-image/side-condition replacements. Node proof fields and a proof-mode def_intro/apply_def attempt exist, but the local proof is merely pinned, not attached to nodes or composed during reconstruction. Every changed normalized formula receives a null proof. Inherited support is false. **Work:** Certify surjectivity/invertibility and restricted images per operator, connect fresh replacements through definitions and proof conversion, store root/ancestor congruence proofs, and transport all normalized assertions to originals. ## Public simplifier catalogue

The same public name can have different tactic and simplifier implementations.
In particular `bit-blast`, `propagate-bv-bounds`, `propagate-values` and
unconstrained elimination must not be merged by name alone.

<!-- BEGIN SIMPLIFIERS -->
**Coverage:** 45 names. **Classes:** 1: 12; 2a: 3; 2b: 0; 3: 15; 4: 15.

| Name | Class | Proof contract and required work | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bit-blast` | **4** | The proof-aware rewriter lowers BV operations to Boolean circuits, but also replaces every uninterpreted BV constant by mkbv of unrelated fresh Booleans. The simplifier composes its returned rewrite proof using mp and installs only model hide/reconstruction entries for those fresh bits. This is a signature-changing equisatisfiable encoding, not a universally valid constant equality; inherited proof support is false. **Work:** Add a proof conversion or certified fresh-bit definitions transporting backend leaves to original constants, then enable the support contract. Ordinary operator circuits can reuse rewrite/congruence infrastructure; constant correspondence cannot be supplied by unconditional mk_rewrite. **Variants:** blast_mul/blast_add limit operator expansion; blast_full uses bit2bool projections for residual terms, which are valid same-signature identities. blast_quant changes bound BV declarations to Boolean binders and needs binder correspondence validation. Constant-free same-signature circuit rewriting is a smaller category-3 subcase. Proof tactic adapter skips the simplifier; unsafe when forced through unchecked custom solver pipeline. ## Internal algorithms and driver boundaries

These entries state the proof boundary actually reviewed. A numeric helper that
does not own assertions need not itself manufacture proofs, but its caller must
retain the coefficients/premises needed to certify the resulting inference.
For theory-rewriter dispatch, the protocol is reviewed; the catalogue does not
claim exhaustive formal correctness of all underlying rewrite identities.

Composite and all three children inherit supports_proofs=false. then_simplifier::reduce nevertheless calls children without component capability checks, and mk_simplifier_solver custom-factory installation is unchecked (parent-owned driver boundary). Only bounded-int extraction has a per-premise gate: bound_manager ignores inputs with nonnull proof, so initially proof-bearing bounds do not trigger encoding. Null-proof inputs and cached bounds remain eligible. Bounded-int replacement discards proof, adds unproved side conditions, and stores only model definitions; card2bv has no internal proof gate, drops first th_rewriter proof and appends null-proof side constraints; enum wrapper has no internal proof gate, uses rewriter proof+MP but appends null-proof enum bounds and model-only definitions. This unsupported pipeline does not claim proof support merely because the unchecked driver forces reduce. **Work:** Immediately refuse or skip the whole unsupported FD pipeline for proof requests, or select a supported preprocessing fallback. Genuine support requires premise-backed bounded integer encoding, certified PB/enum side constraints, and closed inverse theorem transport for fresh declarations and original assertions. Reuse audited dt2bv transport where its enum fragment applies; retain parent-owned solver-driver gate/refutation work separately. **Variants:** All freshly supplied nonnull-proof bound premises make bounded-int stage a no-op because of bound_manager's p gate, not because it supports proofs. Other stages can still mutate. Missing/null original proofs or prior cached bounds can activate bounded encoding even in a proof-enabled manager. Factory always installs all three children; no component-sensitive fallback or proof-parameter branch. Unsafe when forced through unchecked custom solver pipeline. **Evidence status:** source-reviewed. | ## Validation and limitations

Source paths and all cited line ranges were checked against the review checkout.
Public-name completeness was checked against the 170-entry registration/runtime
inventory, including duplicate/omission detection after adapter reconciliation.
There are no unclassified public entries.

Runtime probes loaded **`C:\z3fstar\build\libz3.dll`**, using
`PYTHONPATH=C:\z3fstar\build\install\python` and
`Z3_LIBRARY_PATH=C:\z3fstar\build`. That existing build reports a cached base hash
`dd4179a5500dfe5dea9ab923177f5a4339c4ffd1`, not the review commit. Runtime
observations corroborate the inspected source and concrete countermodels; they
are **not represented as a fresh revision-verified build of this document's
checkout**. No full build or broad regression suite was run for this
documentation-only review.

### Minimal executable witnesses

Use a separate proof-enabled context for each case:

```python
import z3

c = z3.Context(proof=True)
x = z3.Int("x", ctx=c)
s = z3.Simplifier("randomizer", ctx=c).add(z3.Solver(ctx=c))
s.add(x > 0, x < 0)
assert s.check() == z3.unsat
print(s.proof().sexpr())  # old x premise, renamed x_rand rewrite antecedent
```

```python
c = z3.Context(proof=True)
x = z3.Int("x", ctx=c)
f = z3.Function("f", z3.IntSort(c), z3.IntSort(c))
s = z3.Then(z3.Tactic("demodulator", ctx=c),
            z3.Tactic("smt", ctx=c)).solver()
s.add(z3.ForAll(x, f(x) == x), z3.ForAll(x, f(x) > x))
assert s.check() == z3.unsat
print(s.proof().sexpr())
# MP: asserted forall x f(x)>x; elim-unused starts at forall x false.
# The returned proof omits f(x)=x; f(x)=x+1 satisfies its sole input leaf.
```

```python
c = z3.Context(proof=True)
u, v = z3.BitVecs("u v", 4, ctx=c)
fs = [u == 1, v == 0, z3.BV2Int(u) % z3.BV2Int(v) != z3.BV2Int(u)]
plain = z3.Solver(ctx=c)
plain.add(*fs)
assert plain.check() == z3.sat  # e.g. integer mod0 returns 0
encoded = z3.Simplifier("bit2int", ctx=c).add(z3.Solver(ctx=c))
encoded.add(*fs)
assert encoded.check() == z3.unsat  # BV urem(1,0)=1: invalid conversion
print(encoded.proof().sexpr())
```

```python
c = z3.Context(proof=True)
x = z3.Bool("x", ctx=c)
f = z3.Function("f", z3.BoolSort(c), z3.BoolSort(c))
s = z3.Simplifier("pull-nested-quantifiers", ctx=c).add(z3.Solver(ctx=c))
s.add(z3.Exists(x, f(x)), z3.ForAll(x, z3.Not(f(x))))
assert s.check() == z3.unsat
print(s.proof().sexpr())  # MP has exists premise and new-formula reflexivity
```

The additional findings include their own input shapes and observed inference
fragments. No claim is made that a SAT result validates a proof-producing path,
that a no-op exercises the requested transformation, or that missing Pulse rule
coverage proves an error in Z3's producer.

## Recommended order of work

1. Fix the semantic `bit2int` zero-divisor error (including QE's forced use), and
   reject unsound `sk_hack` strengthening. These can change SAT to UNSAT.
2. Repair endpoint/premise logging in `bit2int`, demodulation, quantifier pulling
   and arithmetic purification; add capability/availability checks at drivers.
3. Add original-premise transport for bit blasting, FP encodings and gauge fixing,
   using the closed-substitution/ordinary-converter design audited for `dt2bv`.
4. Enable the category-3 local rewrites and restricted certificate-aware
   frontends before attempting category-4 contextual, elimination and backend
   reconstruction projects.

These are review recommendations, not implementation changes made here.
